--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
The mission is AGGREGATION - minimize the score, which increases when robots are outside the target region. Wait, re-reading: "the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region." The target region is the BLUE-bordered strip with BLACK floor. So robots need to get to the black-floored strip near blue walls and stay there.

Key perceptions:
- Floor color: BLACK = target strip, GRAY = middle area
- Wall colors: BLUE = target side, GREEN = other side
- Camera can detect colored objects (walls)

**Observations from Round 1:**
- Champion uses 3-state exploration with various conditions (score 8832)
- Simple repulsion (1 state) scores ~9501 - repulsion helps spread robots toward walls
- Single-state GoToColor (blue walls) was worst - but that's because it moved toward green too
- The champion uses all Exploration behaviors with transitions based on floor color and neighbor counts

**Strategy:**
The robots need to:
1. Move toward the blue-bordered strip (detect blue walls with camera)
2. Stay on black floor (transition on gray floor to keep moving)
3. Use GoToColor(blue) to navigate toward blue walls

**Design ideas:**
- GoToColor blue walls (clr=3 is blue? Let me check: colors {1,2,3,4,5,6} - likely 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)
- State: GoToColor(blue=3) when not on black floor, Stop or Exploration when on black floor
- Use floor color C=0 (BLACK) to stop, C=1 (GRAY) to move toward blue

**Machines to generate:**
1. SURVIVAL: Champion verbatim
2-6. EXPLOITATION: Small edits of champion
7-14. EXPLORATION: New topologies

For exploitation of champion, I'll tweak probabilities and gains slightly.

For exploration:
- GoToColor(blue) + stop on black floor
- GoToColor(blue) + exploration
- 2-state: GoToColor blue when gray, stop when black
- Repulsion-based with floor transitions
- ZigZag + GoToColor

Note: Color 3 = blue (walls bordering target region). Camera detects colored walls.

FSM designs:

**Exploration machines:**

A) 2-state: State0=GoToColor(blue, clr=3) when GRAY floor; State1=Stop when BLACK floor
B) 2-state: State0=Exploration; transition to GoToColor(blue) on GRAY; back on BLACK  
C) 3-state with GoToColor(blue), Exploration, Stop
D) Simple 1-state repulsion (already tested, ~9501)
E) GoAwayColor(green=2) to move away from green walls

Let me construct 14 machines:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insight from Round 2:**
The champion (score 2102) uses:
- State 0: Exploration (rwm=30) → transitions to GoToColor(blue=3) on BLACK floor OR many neighbors, to Exploration(state0... wait, destination 1) on few neighbors
- State 1: GoToColor(blue, clr=3) → transitions back on many neighbors or GRAY floor
- State 2: Exploration (rwm=87) → transitions on WHITE floor, few neighbors, or BLACK floor

The key mechanism: GoToColor(blue=clr=3) is the core. When robots hit black floor (target strip), they switch to GoToColor(blue) which keeps them near the blue walls. When they're in gray area, they explore until finding the black strip.

**Second best (2409):** Simple 2-state: GoToColor(blue) → stop when on BLACK floor; explore when on GRAY.

**Third best (3273):** GoAwayColor(green=2) + Stop on BLACK floor - clever! Moving away from green walls also gets to blue strip.

**Strategy for Round 3:**
1. Survival: Champion verbatim
2. Exploitation: Tweak champion parameters (probabilities, thresholds)
3. Exploration: New combinations building on what works

Key observations:
- GoToColor(blue=3) is very effective
- GoAwayColor(green=2) also works
- BLACK floor detection triggers staying behavior
- clr values: blue walls = 3 (confirmed by champion)

Note: LED colors must be 0, 4, 5, or 6 only (fixing previous error).

Let me design 14 machines:

1. Champion (verbatim)
2-6. Exploitations of champion
7-8. Exploitations of 2nd best (simple GoToColor + explore)
9. GoAwayColor(green) variant
10-14. New explorations

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 3 results:**

The new champion (score 1698.64) uses a 3-state machine:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(green=2) → go to BLACK floor (p=0.9) or random (p=0.05)
- State 1: Stop → go to GRAY floor (p=0.8) or random (p=0.02)  
- State 2: GoToColor(blue=3) → go to BLACK floor (p=0.9) or GRAY floor (p=0.7)

This is elegant: robots flee green walls (moving toward blue side), stop when on black (target), use GoToColor(blue) to fine-tune positioning, return to stop on black.

Wait, let me re-read:
- State 0 (GoAwayColor green): on BLACK→go to state1(Stop); random→stay
- State 1 (Stop): on GRAY→go to state0; random→go to state1... wait destinations...

With 3 states, leaving state 0: others are [1,2] so D=0→state1, D=1→state2
- State 0→D=0(state1) on BLACK; D=1(state1) random... both go to state1? No wait --n0x1 1 means D=1 which is state2.

So: State0(GoAwayGreen) → State1(Stop) on BLACK | State2(GoToBlue) on random
State1(Stop) → State0(GoAwayGreen) on GRAY | State1... wait D=0 from state1 is state0, D=1 is state2
- State1→State0 on GRAY; State1→State2 on random
- State2(GoToBlue) → State1(Stop) on BLACK; State0(GoAwayGreen) on GRAY

This creates a nice cycle: explore away from green → stop on black → go to blue if needed.

**Exploitation plan:**
1. Survival: champion verbatim
2. Vary p values: p0x0 (0.9→0.95), p0x1 (0.05→0.03), p1x0 (0.8→0.85 or 0.9)
3. Vary the stop condition in state1 (maybe add neighbor condition)
4. Try GoAwayColor(green) with different transitions
5. Add 4th state variations

**Exploration:**
- Try GoAwayColor(green) → GoToColor(blue) directly (2-state)
- Try combining with exploration state
- Try different clr values

Note: LED colors must be 0, 4, 5, 6 only.

FSM: --nstates 3 --s0 9 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 5 --clr0 2 --n0 2 --n0x0 1 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.9 --n0x1 1 --c0x1 5 --p0x1 0.05 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 2 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.8 --n1x1 1 --c1x1 5 --p1x1 0.02 --s2 8 --vel2 1.0 --cle2 0 --clr2 3 --n2 2 --n2x0 1 --c2x0 0 --p2x0 0.9 --n2x1 0 --c2x1 1 --p2x1 0.7

Exploitation variants:

**Variant A:** p0x0=0.95, p1x0=0.85
**Variant B:** p0x1=0.03, p2x1=0.6  
**Variant C:** p1x0=0.9, p2x0=0.95
**Variant D:** Add neighbor condition to state1
**Variant E:** p0x0=0.85, p2x0=0.85

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 5:**

The champion (1290.02) remains unchanged. The best performers are all very close variants of the same 3-state structure:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(green=2) → Stop(state1) on BLACK | GoToColor(blue, state2) when perceiving blue
- State 1: Stop → GoAwayColor(state0) on GRAY | GoToColor(state2) random
- State 2: GoToColor(blue=3) → Stop(state1) on BLACK | GoAwayColor(state0) on GRAY

Key observations:
- Adding a 3rd transition to state0 (C=5 random, C=7 perceive blue) helps (score 1307)
- Adding perception of green walls in state2 (C=7, l=2) helps (score 1321)
- p values around 0.85-0.95 for BLACK transitions work well
- 4-state machines performed poorly
- Stop state with extra BLACK transition hurts

**Exploitation plan:**
Focus on fine-tuning the best performers. The key parameters to vary:
- p0x0 (BLACK prob): 0.85-0.95
- p0x1 (perceive blue prob): 0.75-0.90
- p1x0 (GRAY prob): 0.75-0.85
- p2x0 (BLACK prob): 0.88-0.95
- p2x1 (GRAY prob): 0.65-0.75
- Adding/removing transitions
- Combining features from top 2 (3-transition state0 + green perception in state2)

**Key combination to try:** 3-transition state0 (BLACK + perceive_blue + random) + 3-transition state2 (BLACK + GRAY + perceive_green)

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 7:**

The champion (1290.02) still holds. Very close variants are scoring around 1298-1353. Key findings:

1. **p2x1=0.65** (GRAY prob in state2) is slightly better than 0.7 (rank 1: 1298 vs champion's 1290)
2. **p1x0=0.75** (GRAY prob in state1) is slightly worse than 0.8 (rank 2: 1339)
3. **p0x1=0.75** for camera blue perception works similarly (rank 3: 1339)
4. **Adding random transition to state2** (p2x2=0.03) helps slightly (rank 4: 1348)
5. Adding GRAY transition to state0 hurts (worst 2)
6. Adding 4 transitions to state0 hurts (worst 3)

**What to try:**
- Combine p2x1=0.65 with other small tweaks
- Try p2x1=0.6 (further reduction)
- Try p1x0=0.77-0.78 (between 0.75 and 0.8)
- Try p0x1=0.77 for camera
- Try state2 with random transition + p2x1=0.65
- Carefully vary p0x0 around 0.9

**Avoid:**
- Adding GRAY to state0
- 4 transitions in state0
- p2x0=0.93 (too high)
- Replacing C=5 in state1 with camera conditions

**Strategy:** Focus tightly on the champion topology with fine-grained parameter search around the optimum. The structure is locked in; only probabilities matter now.

Key parameters to tune:
- p0x0: 0.88-0.93 (BLACK in state0)
- p0x1: 0.75-0.85 (camera blue in state0)
- p1x0: 0.75-0.82 (GRAY in state1)
- p1x1: 0.015-0.025 (random in state1)
- p2x0: 0.88-0.92 (BLACK in state2)
- p2x1: 0.60-0.70 (GRAY in state2)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 10 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.62 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
